# Platinum move animation requirements and VM coverage

Implementation update (2026-10-16): generic sprite resources, all 25 callbacks in the 29-move batch, and the seven single-command wins now run on the VM. Actual-move fallbacks are **9/467** (previously 45); **458/467** play. The tables below retain the original audit baseline. Sprite choreography/native timing remain approximate; ROM execution and browser pixel tests cover the implementation. Remaining moves: Surf, Night Shade, Transform, Acid Armor, Sketch, Spite, Role Play, Extrasensory and Muddy Water.

Audited 2026-10-16 against OpenSinnoh `60b31aba587c6d3092390606a3d9b78ffbf581e4`.

**422 of 467 actual Platinum moves pass animation preflight; 45 fall back.** The older 422/468 figure includes
archive slot 0 (`MOVE_NONE`), which is also blocked. All required SPA members are present; the blockers are
11 opcodes, 20 native functions and 25 generic sprite callbacks. Seven individual command implementations
would each unlock one move immediately. Generic sprite infrastructure is the largest shared dependency:
31 moves need it, but its individual loader commands unlock zero moves without their other requirements.

## Scope, sources and counting

The primary requirements source is Platinum's move animation assembly, linked for every move below at
decomp revision `1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20`. The [move enum](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/generated/moves.txt) and
[archive build order](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/meson.build) define IDs 0–467. The
[particle archive order](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/graphics/battle/particles/battle_particles.order) supplies SPA names.
The decomp is used only for names and understanding; this note contains requirement sets, not copied
scripts, source implementations, assets or ROM bytes.

Requirements were measured by walking the local US ROM's first 468 `/wazaeffect/we.arc` members with the
engine's [`scriptUse` traversal](../../packages/battle/src/anim/script.ts). A separate instruction
walk checked the same opcode, native-function, sprite-callback and SPA sets against `scriptUse`, and the
blocker sets against [`animSupport`](../../packages/battle/src/anim/index.ts). Every script's SPA set
was also cross-checked against its decomp source, resolving symbolic names through the particle order;
all 468 matched. No ROM bytes or decomp code are copied; the per-move table lists requirement sets derived from the decomp sources and our ROM walk.

- **Main counts:** distinct actual moves, IDs 1–467, with an opposing-side target. Both attacker sides,
  all effect-chance/charge-turn and weather branches, and called subroutines are included. A requirement
  repeated in a loop counts once per move. Counts are static potential paths, not one sampled battle.
- **Ally targets:** the engine selects the friendly-fire arm instead. Nine scripts have different
  instructions, listed below; none changes its preflight blockers or playable/fallback classification.
  This is a script audit, not a claim that every move can legally target an ally.
- **Contests:** excluded from battle unlock counts. Additional contest-reachable requirements are
  recorded separately below. The battle VM never takes `JumpIfContest`.
- **Unlock alone:** this command is the move's only missing requirement, assuming a complete normal
  import. **Potential unlocks:** moves using the missing command; they unlock only after every other
  blocker in their row is implemented. Potential counts overlap and must not be added.
- `JumpIfEqual`/`JumpIfUnk_01` absolute-pointer targets are not walked by `scriptUse`; neither command
  occurs on these production battle paths. Unreachable padding/test scripts and the 50 common
  animations are outside the 467-move totals.

The [importer](../../packages/importer/src/battle/anim.ts) imports all 485 SPA systems, all 58
switchable backgrounds and the animation tables. Preflight checks SPA presence and exactly one
`battleAnimData` record, but does not verify every background sheet or sprite resource. Thus “unlocked”
here means the current preflight would accept the script after the named implementations; it still
requires implementation tests and the appropriate assets.

The five sprite setup/load opcodes have identical 31-move coverage. `AddSpriteWithFunc` is needed by
29 of them; Surf and Muddy Water use `AddSprite` with native functions instead. All 25 sprite callback
kinds in this table are missing. `FreeSpriteManager` is already accepted, but currently does no cleanup
work because no generic sprites are allocated. An implementation must replace that stub as well.

### Complete dependency bundles

The following disjoint batches cover all 45 blocked moves, ordered by batch size. Each batch gives the
full missing-command union needed for its moves. The sprite asset pipeline is an additional prerequisite,
not represented by the preflight command count.
| Batch | New moves | Complete missing set |
|---|---:|---|
| Generic sprites and callbacks | 29 | `InitSpriteManager`, `LoadCharResObj`, `LoadPlttRes`, `LoadCellResObj`, `LoadAnimResObj`, `AddSpriteWithFunc`; all 25 `SpriteFunc_…` callbacks listed above; `Func_Swagger`, `Func_FakeOut`, `Func_FakeOutCurtain`. Import and draw the corresponding NCGR/NCLR/NCER/NANR resources (listed below). |
| Single-command wins | 7 | `Func_BattlerPartialDraw`, `Func_Camouflage`, `Func_FaintAttack`, `Func_Memento`, `Func_Mimic`, `Func_Snatch`, `StartBattlerSlideIn` — 91 Dig, 102 Mimic, 164 Substitute, 185 Faint Attack, 262 Memento, 289 Snatch, 293 Camouflage |
| Battler copied into BG | 4 | `Func_AcidArmor`, `Func_Extrasensory`, `Func_Sketch`, `Func_Spite`, `LoadPokemonSpriteIntoBg`, `RemovePokemonSpriteFromBg` — 151 Acid Armor, 166 Sketch, 180 Spite, 326 Extrasensory |
| Slide and transform tasks | 2 | `Func_PixelatePokemonSprite`, `Func_RolePlay`, `StartBattlerSlideIn`, `StartBattlerSlideOut` — 144 Transform, 272 Role Play |
| Surf and Muddy Water | 2 | `AddSprite`, `Func_MuddyWater`, `Func_ScrollCustomBg`, `Func_Surf`, `InitSpriteManager`, `LoadAnimResObj`, `LoadCellResObj`, `LoadCharResObj`, `LoadPlttRes` — 57 Surf, 330 Muddy Water |
| Night Shade | 1 | `Func_NightShadeAttacker`, `Func_NightShadeDefender` — 101 Night Shade |

The BG pair alone unlocks zero moves: Acid Armor, Sketch, Spite and Extrasensory each also need their
native function. `StartBattlerSlideIn` alone unlocks Substitute; Transform additionally needs
`Func_PixelatePokemonSprite`, and Role Play needs `StartBattlerSlideOut` and `Func_RolePlay`.
Night Shade needs both its native functions. Implementing these batches would raise 422/467 to 467/467
under the existing gate; it would not establish pixel-exact rendering.

## What is already supported

The declared implementation sets are [`ANIM_OPCODES`](../../packages/battle/src/anim/vm.ts)
(68 of 85 defined opcodes) and [`ANIM_FUNCS`](../../packages/battle/src/anim/funcs.ts)
(56 of 84 defined native functions). On the 467 normal battle paths, 44 of the 55 used opcodes and
52 of the 72 used native functions are accepted. All 23 emitter callback IDs, 0–22, are handled by
[`createEmitterCallback`](../../packages/battle/src/anim/callbacks.ts); these are separate from the
unsupported `SpriteFunc_…` callbacks.

The tables below give each **used** command's distinct-move count. Unused supported entries need no
implementation work; unused missing entries have zero Platinum battle unlocks and follow afterward.

These are zero only for this move inventory: some are used by common status/item animations or test

introduce no extra missing command kinds.

## Visual fidelity and fallback limits

The [controller](../../packages/battle/src/controller.ts) logs `anim:<move>` and returns
without starting the move VM if any requirement is missing. Its log calls this “the generic hit”.
The actual [damage processing](../../packages/battle/src/controller.ts) independently schedules
the [12-tick blink/shake reaction](../../packages/battle/src/controller.ts), including after
supported animations. An unsupported status move therefore has no move scene and need not have any
generic damage reaction. Options “battle scene off” skips move scenes deliberately.

Accepted commands can still approximate the cartridge:

- SPL polygon particles are drawn as billboards; perspective projection is drawn orthographically.
  Canvas alpha approximates BG/OBJ blending; sprite/BG priority and sound panning are not faithfully
  reproduced. See the original animation decision in `docs/decisions.md` and
  [the animation renderer](../../packages/app/src/battle/anim/draw.ts).
- `Func_ShakeBg` has incorrect layer selection, marked **≈** in the BG column. Platinum's
  [target constants](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/include/constants/battle/battle_anim.h#L229) mean 0 = effect and 1 = base;
  an omitted sixth argument becomes zero. The VM applies target 0 to a shared offset affecting both
  base and effect, while target 1 draws no shake and logs a misleading switched-layer message
  (`anim:shake-bg:base`). Thunder explicitly requests the base (1), so its base shake is skipped.
  BG cells describe the source's required layer. See
  [the native implementation](../../packages/battle/src/anim/funcs.ts),
  [the shared offset](../../packages/battle/src/anim/bg.ts) and
  [both layer draw sites](../../packages/app/src/battle/scene.ts).
- `Func_SetPokemonSpritePriority` is accepted, but Dark Void's nonzero sinking mode logs
  `anim:SetPokemonSpritePriority:mode` and leaves the copy fixed. `Func_Superpower` times the scene
  without drawing its window effect. See [sprite functions](../../packages/battle/src/anim/funcs3.ts).
- `Func_FadeBg` is accepted for base/effect palettes, but its battler-BG mode throws. Porting the
  battler-to-BG commands must also account for that argument mode. The gate does
  not model argument-level support. Missing switched BG art similarly fails only when drawing it.

Normal and double battles use these scripts today; the earlier note's statement that doubles were
unsupported is obsolete. Contest presentation remains outside this battle VM.

## Every move: SPA, special backgrounds and commands

Move IDs are the `/wazaeffect/we.arc` member indices. Each move name links to its complete primary-source
animation script. **✓** means accepted by preflight; **✗** means missing. “Plays” describes gate acceptance,
with the fidelity limits above. “Fallback” means the entire move script is rejected.

SPA entries are archive member ID plus file name, not the move ID: moves often share particle systems.
All SPA entries in this table are imported and supported by the resource gate. **—** means no direct
requirement of that kind; a native function can still generate a screen effect internally. The BG column
lists switched background indices, blend/fade modes, scroll/wave flags, palette fades, grayscale,
shakes and battler-to-BG copies. BG numbers identify the 0–57 animation background table, not SPA IDs
or raw NARC member numbers. Restore operations and native custom BG requests are included.

### Command shorthand

**P** is a supported *set shorthand*, not execution order:
`AddPokemonSprite`, `CallFunc`, `FreePokemonSpriteManager`, `InitPokemonSpriteManager`, `LoadParticleSystem`, `LoadPokemonSpriteDummyResources`, `RemovePokemonSprite`, `WaitForAnimTasks`, plus `Func_RenderPokemonSprites`. Each P use below includes all of
these; all other required opcodes/functions/callbacks are written explicitly. This expands the
`LoadParticleResource` macro to VM commands, so it also covers implicit sprite setup. Commands are
sets, with repeated calls and operands omitted. The decomp may spell opcode 82/83 as `BtlAnimCmd_082`/
`BtlAnimCmd_083`; this note uses the VM names `StartPokemonSpriteDrawTask`/`StopPokemonSpriteDrawTask`.
| ID / move / source | Gate | SPA members (all ✓) | Special BG requirements | Commands used |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1 Pound](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/pound/anim.s) | Plays | 31 `pound.spa` | — | [✓ P](#command-shorthand), ✓ `CreateEmitter`, ✓ `End`, ✓ `PlayPannedSoundEffect`, ✓ `UnloadParticleSystem`, ✓ `WaitForAllEmitters`, ✓ `Func_Shake` |
| [2 Karate Chop](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/karate_chop/anim.s) | Plays | 32 `karate_chop.spa` | — | [✓ P](#command-shorthand), ✓ `CreateEmitter`, ✓ `End`, ✓ `PlayDelayedSoundEffect`, ✓ `PlayPannedSoundEffect`, ✓ `UnloadParticleSystem`, ✓ `WaitForAllEmitters`, ✓ `Func_Shake` |
| [3 DoubleSlap](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/double_slap/anim.s) | Plays | 33 `double_slap.spa` | — | [✓ P](#command-shorthand), ✓ `CreateEmitter`, ✓ `Delay`, ✓ `End`, ✓ `PlayPannedSoundEffect`, ✓ `UnloadParticleSystem`, ✓ `WaitForAllEmitters`, ✓ `Func_Shake` |

### Slot 0 (not a move)

[0 None](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/none/anim.s) requires 186 `sketch.spa`; battler→BG (role 1, mode 0); remove battler BG. Its commands are [✓ P](#command-shorthand), ✓ `CreateEmitter`, ✓ `End`, ✗ `LoadPokemonSpriteIntoBg`, ✓ `PlayLoopedSoundEffect`, ✗ `RemovePokemonSpriteFromBg`, ✓ `UnloadParticleSystem`, ✓ `WaitForAllEmitters`, ✗ `Func_Sketch`.
It falls back on `LoadPokemonSpriteIntoBg`, `RemovePokemonSpriteFromBg` and `Func_Sketch`. This is the
46th blocked archive slot but is excluded from every move-unlock count.

### Generic sprite resources needed by the 31-move batch

The [current animation importer](../../packages/importer/src/battle/anim.ts) imports scripts,
SPA systems and BG sheets, not the generic move sprite resource archives. Loading these records and
drawing their NCGR/NCLR/NCER/NANR animation is part of unlocking the sprite batch. The following names
are from each linked move source (an asset checklist, not copied assets). Numeric entries are literal
resource indices where the source has no symbolic name.

## Ally-target and contest differences

These compare reachable instruction sets, not a particular run. “+” means an additional requirement;
“−” means a requirement on the normal path that is absent on the alternate path. SPA and BG entries
listed here are also imported. An “emitter selection only” change alters emitter IDs/placement branches
without changing the listed resource or command sets.

| [57 Surf](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum/blob/1dc13dcea40c04ab53f1f607de46023dc409ef20/res/moves/surf/anim.s) | + opcode: JumpIfBattlerSide | Unchanged |

The contest union follows the jump arm as well as the battle fallthrough; it is a requirements
superset, not a claim that the battle VM implements contests. Generic sprite callback and opcode
support is unchanged. `SwitchBgEx`, if introduced by future scripts, also has a contest BG operand
which the current VM does not select.

## Formats, timing and approximations (M2b reference)

Kept from the M2b note this file replaces. Sources: decomp `src/battle_anim/*.c`,
`include/data/scripts/btlanimcmd.h`, `asm/macros/btlanimcmd.inc`, `btlanimfunc.inc`; NitroSDK SPL
(names only).

### Files and overlay tables
- `we_sub.narc` holds the 50 common animations; our names follow the decomp file names (`poison`,
  `stat_boost`, `weather_rain` …). Nine are empty; 37 of the 41 non-empty ones play for real.
- `waza_particle.narc`: 485 SPA files, 1,468 emitters, 1,437 textures (A3I5 and A5I3 mostly; many tiled
  and mirrored).
- Overlay 12 tables (SHA-1 checked at import): battler world positions (0x1a640,
  [type][kind][projection]), the eight emitter axis tables (0x193cc), the switched backgrounds (0x18d08,
  58 × 5 NARC members) and the stat-change patterns (0x1a5b8, 4 × 4).

### Script semantics
- `CallFunc` (func, argc, args → script vars 0–9, the rest zeroed). `SetExtraParams` (count, words) is
  read inline by the preceding `CreateEmitter`'s callback; a top-level one is a decomp `GF_ASSERT`.
- `JumpIfBattlerSide` takes operand 3 for the enemy's side, 2 otherwise. `JumpIfEffectChance(Odd)` reads
  the effect chance the battle passes (charge turn 0, attack turn 1).
- `Delay n` holds n frames plus one switch frame (scriptDelay + waitMode). `WaitForAnimTasks`,
  `WaitForAllEmitters`, `WaitForBgSwitch` poll each frame. `End` gives one frame of grace, then waits for
  emitters, anim tasks, sound tasks, and a playing SE for at most 90 frames.
- Frame order in SysTask priority: script (0) → anim and sound tasks (1100, including tasks started this
  frame) → BG scroll tasks → draw (sprite fades step; particles drawn, then updated) → palette fade.

### Particles (SPA / SPL)
- Header 32 bytes; per emitter an 88-byte resource header plus optional blocks chosen by flag bits:
  scale anim (8), colour anim (9), alpha anim (10), texture anim (11), child (16), and the behaviours
  gravity (24), random (25), magnet (26), spin (27), collision plane (28), convergence (29). Other bits:
  emission type 0–3, draw type 4–5, circle axis 6–7, random init angle 13, self-maintaining 14, follow
  emitter 15, poly rotation axis 17–18, poly reference plane 19, randomise looped anim 20, draw children
  first 21, hide parent 22, view space 23. Misc words at 0x44/0x48/0x4C pack interval, alpha, air,
  texture, loop frames, DBB scale, tiling, scale direction, face emitter, flips.
- RNG: `state * 0x5eedf715 + 0x1b0cb173`. Pools: 20 emitters, 200 particles per system; lists newest
  first. A non-self-maintaining emitter lives until deleted (the movers delete theirs).
- `CreateEmitter(ps, resource, callback)`: the callback (23 kinds) places the emitter on a battler's world
  position, sets axes from the tables and per-move behaviours from `SetExtraParams`. The generic callback
  edits the system's (copied) behaviour data.
- Camera: at (0, 0, 4) looking at the origin, orthographic, 24 DS px per unit, screen centre (128, 96);
  `SetCameraFlip` toggles up.y on each `CreateEmitter`.

### Backgrounds and sprites
- `SwitchBg(bg, mode | flags << 16)`: mode 0 blends the new background in (EVA 0→29, EVB 31→2 by 2s),
  mode 1 fades through black or white, mode 2 sets flags only. Flags: move (scroll by vars 0–3), wave
  (16 strips of HBlank scroll). Screen/anim reversal (vars 6, 7) mirror for the player's side.
- Palette fades (`PaletteData_StartFade`): one fade at a time, steps of 2 (or 2 − wait for a negative
  wait) every wait + 1 frames. PokemonSprite fades step ±1 every delay + 1 frames in the draw task.
- `AddPokemonSprite` makes an OAM copy of a battler (afterimages, Double Team, the stat arrows' window).

### What M2b built, and approximations
- Import: scripts, all 485 particle systems (full SPA reader, textures through the shared 2D decoders),
  the overlay tables, switched backgrounds and stat patterns as `ui2d` sheets.
- `packages/battle/src/anim`: the VM (headless, deterministic, `AnimView` out), SPL particles, the 23
  emitter callbacks, background switching and fades.
- Coverage: **422 / 468 move scripts (90.2 %) play for real** counting slot 0, which is the 422 / 467
  real moves above (slot 0 is the 46th fallback). A script plays only if the VM implements all of it;
  otherwise the generic hit plays and the missing pieces are logged (`anim:<move>`).
- Approximations: polygon draw types are drawn as billboards; the perspective projection (rare) is drawn
  orthographic; sound pan is not applied; BG/OBJ blending uses canvas alpha (EVA/EVB weights); sprite
  priority against BG layers is ignored; contests are not supported. (Doubles now play; see above.)

## Runtime and reproduction notes

- Canonical ROM paths are `/wazaeffect/we.arc`, `/wazaeffect/we_sub.narc` and
  `/wazaeffect/effectdata/waza_particle.narc`; background art comes from
  `/battle/graphic/pl_batt_bg.narc`. There are 468 imported move slots and 50 common slots.
  See [the importer and verified overlay-table offsets](../../packages/importer/src/battle/anim.ts).
- Scripts use 32-bit words and 85 opcode IDs. `CallFunc`, `SetExtraParams` and `AddSpriteWithFunc`
  have variable operand counts. `SetExtraParams` belongs to its preceding emitter callback; a
  top-level invocation is an internal error. Jumps are relative to the operand word.
- One simulation tick is one 30 Hz battle frame: script, animation/sound tasks, BG scrolls, draw and
  particle update, then palette fade. `Delay n` includes its switch frame; particle loading waits two
  ticks. End waits for tasks/emitters and caps waiting for a playing SE at 90 ticks. See
  [VM timing](../../packages/battle/src/anim/vm.ts),
  [background transitions](../../packages/battle/src/anim/bg.ts) and
  [SPL simulation](../../packages/battle/src/anim/spl.ts).
- To reproduce coverage with the shared ROM import, run
  `OPENSINNOH_NDS_ROM="$PWD/Pokemon - Platinum Version (USA).nds" pnpm vitest run packages/battle/src/anim/anim.rom.test.ts`.
  For exact counts, load `romSlice().pack` through `loadPacks`, exclude `common` records and
  `sourceIndex === 0`, and call `animSupport`/`scriptUse` per animation. Invert each distinct missing
  set for the potential counts; count a move as an immediate unlock only if its missing set has size
  one. Repeat with `friendlyFire = true` for ally paths.
- For BG inventory, walk the same reachable PCs and collect `SwitchBg`/`RestoreBg` operands
  (mode = low 16 bits, scroll = high-half bit `0x02`, wave = `0x20`), battler-BG operations and the BG
  native functions. Resolve SPA members using the pinned particle order and verify each move source.
  `LoadParticleResource` is a convenience macro, not an additional VM opcode.

This file changes research documentation only; it does not change importer output or VM support.
